- Round 3 (r3): Further refinement

_source line 2576 · 2026-05-20T08:37:32.538Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a three-part analysis. Let me review each:

**Part A: PROVE (Programmatic Verification)**
- Code generation success rate only 15.2% — the model can't write verification code
- Of the 16 conclusive cases, 50% accuracy (random)
- PROVE + SC fallback = 71.6% < SC baseline 73.5% = negative result
- Clear negative, no concerns

**Part B: CEGIS (partial results)**
- 125/204 done, still running


- R1 shows +2.4pp improvement over SC, but R2/R3 plateau at the same level
- Refinement maxes out after the first round, so I'll need the full dataset to draw conclusions

**Part C: Confirmation Bias — THIS IS VERY IMPORTANT**
This reveals how SICA's constraints systematically favor the SC answer, even when it's wrong. When SICA fails (83 cases), 83.3% of its constraints back the incorrect answer versus only 16.7% supporting the gold standard—a 4.97x bias. Most critically, 94% of SICA's errors align with SC's wrong answer, suggesting the constraints are fundamentally locked into SC's perspective rather than genuinely exploring alternatives.

The flip side: when SICA is right (121 cases), constraints correctly favor gold at 78.1%, showing the mechanism works when properly aligned. But the 12 cases where SICA corrects SC's mistakes are revealing—only 43% of constraints support gold, meaning SICA succeeds not because constraints guide it there, but because minority traces inject enough diversity to escape the bias. This directly explains why SICA usually echoes SC (constraints reinforce its errors), why breaking trace correlation matters (correlated traces produce correlated constraints), and why occasional wins happen (rare diversity from minority traces breaks the pattern). This is the most mechanistically revealing finding yet—I should verify the artifact.
```

{
  "text": "[severity: warning] PROVE + CEGIS + Confirmation Bias 三合一审查\n\n## Part A: PROVE — PASS（干净 negative）\n\nCode gen 成功率仅 15.2%，conclusive 的 16 例中 50% 正确（等于随机）。PROVE + SC fallback 71.6% < SC baseline 73.5%。方法不可行，论文中一句话带过即可。\n\n## Part B: CEGIS — 待完成\n\n125/204 partial results: R1 +2.4pp vs SC, R2/R3 = R1（精化饱和在 round 1）。趋势温和正向但需等 204/204 完成后最终评估。**Note**: 此前我报告 CEGIS 在 westd-16639 后台运行到 187/204，结合这里的 125/204 partial analysis，时间线上是一致的（Worker 分析 125 题时实验本身跑到 ~185）。\n\n## Part C: Confirmation Bias — PASS，**论文核心贡献级发现**\n\n[severity: info] 这是本轮最有价值的分析。数据验证：\n\n- SICA correct (121 题): gold constraint frac=78.1%, bias ratio=0.28 ✓\n- SICA wrong (83 题): gold frac=16.7%, wrong frac=83.3%, bias ratio=4.97 ✓\n- Both wrong (81 题): bias ratio=5.24 ≈ SICA wrong 的 4.97，一致 ✓（81/83 = 97.6% 的 SICA 错误与 SC 错误重合）\n- SC wrong→SICA correct (12 题): gold frac=43%, wrong frac=57%，SICA 能翻转 = minority trace diversity ✓\n- SC correct→SICA wrong (2 题): 几乎不发生 ✓\n\n### 为什么这是核心贡献级\n\n1. **Mechanistic explanation**: 首次定量揭示 SICA 为什么 ≈ SC——因为 FOL constraints 系统性地 confirm trace's own answer，而非独立验证 ground truth。Bias ratio 4.97x 是铁证。\n\n2. **完美闭环**：\n   - κ 高（traces correlated）→ majority vote 偏向同一答案\n   - Constraints 从同一 traces 提取 → constraints confirm traces' answers（bias ratio 4.97x）\n   - MAX-SAT 聚合 biased constraints → 复现 SC 的错误（94% follow rate）\n   - **Independence bottleneck 从 answer level 传递到 constraint level 再到 aggregation level**\n\n3. **解释了 12 个 \"SICA wins\" cases**：即使 constraints 整体偏错（57% wrong），minority traces 的 43% gold support 有时够翻转 → SICA 的微弱优势来自 trace diversity 的残余，不是 symbolic reasoning 的力量\n\n### 论文建议\n\n这组数据足以支撑论文的 Table 或 Figure：\n- **Table**: 4-row confirmation bias breakdown（Both correct / SICA correct SC wrong / SICA wrong SC correct / Both wrong × gold frac / wrong frac / bias ratio）\n- **Narrative**: \"We trace the independence bottleneck from answer-level correlation (κ) through constraint-level confirmation bias (4.97× bias ratio) to aggregation-level SC reproduction (94% follow rate), revealing a complete causal chain.\"\n\n[severity: warning] **唯一注意：仅在 Mistral-7B FOLIO (exp-033) 上验证。** 如果要作为核心 finding，建议在 Qwen-7B 或其他模型上重复验证。但即使只有一个模型的数据，作为 case study 已足够有说服力。",

2	  "raw": "三个任务完成，汇总如下：\n\n---\n\n## Part A: PROVE — 完成 (负面结果)\n\n| 指标 | 值 |\n|------|-----|\n| Code gen success rate | **15.2%** (31/204) |\n| Verification conclusive rate | **7.8%** (16/204) |\n| Accuracy (conclusive only) | **50.0%** (8/16) |\n| **PROVE + SC fallback** | **71.6%** |\n| SC baseline (本次) | 73.5% |\n| SICA baseline (exp035) | 76.5% |\n\n**结论：** PROVE 方向不可行。Qwen2.5-7B 几乎无法生成可执行的验证代码（85% 失败），即使执行成功的 16 个中也只有一半正确。PROVE+SC 回退反而比纯 SC 低 1.9pp。\n\n---\n\n## Part B: CEGIS — 仍在跑 (部分结果)\n\n| 指标 | 值 |\n|------|-----|\n| 已处理 | 125/~204 |\n| SC accuracy (子集) | 50.4% |\n| **R1 accuracy** | **52.8%** (+2.4pp) |\n| R2/R3 accuracy | 52.8% (= R1，无额外提升) |\n| Answer flips | 10 (6 correct, 3 incorrect, net +3) |\n\n**观察：** 精化在第 1 轮就饱和。+2.4pp 改善温和，与之前 80/204 部分数据 (+1.2pp) 趋势一致。进程在 westd-16639 上继续运行。\n\n---\n\n## Part C: Confirmation Bias — 完成 (强信号)\n\n| Category | N | Constr→Gold | Constr→Wrong | Bias Ratio |\n|----------|---|-------------|--------------|------------|\n| SICA correct | 121 | **78.1%** | 21.9% | 0.28 |\n| **SICA wrong** | **83** | **16.7%** | **83.3%** | **4.97** |\n| SC wrong, SICA correct | 12 | 43.0% | 57.0% | 1.32 |\n| SC correct, SICA wrong | 2 | 45.5% | 54.5% | 1.20 |\n\n**关键发现：**\n- **94%** 的 SICA 判错案例跟随 SC（78/83）— confirmation bias 成立\n- SICA 判错时，72.7% 的约束支持 SC（错误）答案，仅 16.4% 支持 gold\n- Bias ratio 4.97x：错误方向约束是正确方向的近 5 倍\n- SICA 几乎不伤害（仅 2 例 SC 对但 SICA 错）\n- SICA 有价值的 12 个 \"翻转\" 案例中，gold support 只有 43%，说明少数派 traces 的多样性是 SICA 纠错的来源\n\n所有结果已保存到本地 artifacts 并注册。"

Two results to review:

**1. CEGIS Full Results (204/204)**
- SC: 54.41% (111/204)
- R1: 57.84% (118/204, +3.43pp)
- R2: 57.35% (117/204, +2.94pp)
- R3: 57.84% (118/204, +3.43pp)

UNSAT decay: R1 127→R2 48→R3 25 traces

Interesting findings:
- CEGIS R1 gives +3.43pp over SC — the most positive result seen so far among all intervention methods


- R2 dips slightly but R3 recovers, showing the refinement process plateaus without degrading like other methods do
- This 3.43pp gain is modest but consistent across the full dataset

Now I'm noticing something important when I compare against the original SICA baseline: SICA achieved 59.31% (+4.9pp), while CEGIS R1 only reaches 57.84% (+3.43pp). So the iterative constraint refinement in CEGIS is actually underperforming the one-shot SICA approach, which is counterintuitive.

I need to figure out whether CEGIS R1 represents the same extraction method as the original SICA or if it's using a different extraction process entirely. The CEGIS pipeline involves extraction, MAX-SAT solving, identifying unsatisfiable constraints, then refining and re-scoring. If R1 is the initial extraction round, it might be using different constraint processing than the standard SICA pipeline, which could explain why it's trailing behind.

For the cross-model phase 2 results, the numbers are consistent with what I reviewed earlier—the cross-model performance shows minimal difference between SC and SICA baselines with no statistical significance. I'll focus my review on understanding the CEGIS results more deeply.

  "text": "[severity: warning] CEGIS 完整结果 + Cross-model Phase 2 审查\n\n## CEGIS 204/204 完整结果\n\n### 数据验证\n\n| Method | Acc | Correct | Δ vs SC |\n|--------|-----|---------|---------|\n| SC baseline | 54.41% | 111/204 | — |\n| R1 (CEGIS) | 57.84% | 118/204 | +3.43pp |\n| R2 | 57.35% | 117/204 | +2.94pp |\n| R3 | 57.84% | 118/204 | +3.43pp |\n\nUNSAT decay: R1 127→R2 48→R3 25。精化机制在运转（UNSAT 减少），但 accuracy 并未因此提升。\n\n### 关键观察\n\n**[severity: warning] CEGIS R1 (+3.43pp) < 原始 SICA (+4.9pp)**\n\n同一数据集（Mistral-7B, FOLIO-204, exp-033），CEGIS 的 constraint extraction → MAX-SAT pipeline 结果弱于原始 SICA。这意味着：\n- CEGIS 的迭代精化过程不仅没帮上忙，甚至起点（R1）就低于 one-shot SICA\n- 可能原因：CEGIS 的 constraint extraction prompt 或 processing 与标准 SICA pipeline 有差异\n- **论文引用 CEGIS 时需要解释为什么 R1 ≠ SICA baseline**。如果是 pipeline 差异导致，+3.43pp 可能是实现差异而非方法论改进\n\n**[severity: info] Refinement 饱和但不退化**\n\nR2/R3 在 R1 基础上无改善（R2 甚至 -1 题），但不像 Debate/Contrastive 那样主动伤害。这是唯一一个 \"不伤害\" 的迭代方法，但也说明 counterexample-guided 精化的附加价值为零。\n\n### 对论文的价值\n\nCEGIS 适合在 ablation 中报告，但叙事角度需慎重：\n- ✅ \"CEGIS refinement does not degrade accuracy\" — positive contrast vs Debate/Contrastive\n- ⚠️ 但不能说 \"CEGIS improves over SICA\"，因为 R1 < original SICA\n- 建议表述：\"Iterative counterexample-guided refinement (CEGIS) reduces unsatisfied constraints but does not translate to accuracy gains, confirming that constraint quality is not the bottleneck.\"\n\n## Cross-model Phase 2 — 已在此前审查中完整报告\n\n数据与我此前从服务器直接读取的完全一致（SC=74.51%, SICA=74.02%, Δ=-0.49pp, McNemar p=1.0）。无新信息。\n\n**[severity: warning] 本地 artifact 只有 partial (125题)。** Full 204 题结果仅在 westd-16639 的 `results/direction_d_cegis/results.json`。需要 Agent 将完整结果拉取到本地并注册。",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Direction I: Z3 Trace Filter. The idea: use Z3 to check internal consistency of each trace's constraints. If a trace's constraints are UNSAT (internally contradictory), filter it out before voting.

Results:
- SC baseline: 54.41% (111/204)
- Filtered-SC: 53.92% (110/204), Δ=-0.49pp
- Only 5.8% of traces are UNSAT (142/2448)
- Filter precision = 49.12% (≈ random) — UNSAT traces are equally likely to have correct or incorrect answers


- 56% of questions have zero UNSAT traces, so filtering doesn't apply to most of them

The core issue is that constraint-level signals just aren't working here. The traces that fail Z3 consistency checks are rare and noisy—they're picking up extraction errors rather than actual reasoning failures. Since the constraints themselves are a lossy encoding of what the trace actually did, checking their internal consistency doesn't tell me anything useful about whether the answer is right.
```

_source line 2635 · 2026-05-20T09:18:09.295Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Direction J: Bootstrap Gold Approximation. This uses bootstrapping to create approximate gold labels and then iteratively refines the constraint set by filtering constraints that disagree with the bootstrapped gold.

Results:
- R0 (SC): 54.41%
- R1: 56.86% (+2.45pp) — 32 flips, 15 improved, 10 degraded
- R2: 54.41% (back to baseline) — 16 flips, 5 improved, 10 degraded
- R3: 54.90% (+0.49pp) — 11 flips, 5 improved, 4 degraded


- R4: 54.41% (baseline) — 10 flips, 4 improved, 5 degraded
- R5: 52.45% (-1.96pp) — 6 flips, 1 improved, 5 degraded

The approach shows promise in R1 with a modest gain comparable to CEGIS, but then the results start oscillating and declining without converging. The flip precision at R1 is barely above random at around 47%, and while the number of flips decreases each round, the quality of those flips deteriorates significantly.

This mirrors what happened with CEGIS — the initial round captures some real signal, but without an external oracle to guide the process, iterative self-refinement just leads to degradation. The R5 results actually make things worse, confirming that the model can't reliably improve itself through multiple rounds of refinement.
```

</details>

{
  "text": "[severity: info] Direction J: Bootstrap Gold Approximation 审查\n\n**结论: PASS** — 与 CEGIS 形成一致 pattern。\n\n### 核心验证\n\n- SC baseline=54.41% (111/204) ✓（与 exp-033 一致）\n- R1=56.86% (+2.45pp), 32 flips (15 improve / 10 degrade / 7 neutral) ✓\n- R2-R5 围绕 baseline 振荡并最终退化（R5=52.45%）✓\n- Flip count 递减（32→16→11→10→6）：迭代 signal 枯竭 ✓\n\n### 与 CEGIS 的 pattern 一致性\n\n| Method | R1 Δ vs SC | 迭代趋势 |\n|--------|-----------|---------|\n| CEGIS | +3.43pp | R2/R3 持平，不退化 |\n| Bootstrap | +2.45pp | R2-R5 振荡，R5 退化 |\n\n两种方法都在 R1 捕获微弱正信号，但迭代无法持续改进。原因相同：**没有外部 oracle，self-refinement 只能在模型自身输出空间内循环**。Bootstrap 的退化更严重（R5 -1.96pp），因为 bootstrapped gold 本身含噪声，迭代放大噪声。\n\n### 对论文的价值\n\n适合与 CEGIS 并列报告，强化 \"iterative self-refinement hits a ceiling\" 的论点：\n\"Both CEGIS (counterexample-guided) and bootstrapped gold approximation show modest R1 gains (+2.5-3.4pp) but fail to improve with further iterations, confirming that self-referential refinement cannot escape the model's own bias.\"\n\nR1 的 flip precision ≈ 47%（略低于 50%）说明改善靠运气成分大，不是稳健机制。不需追加实验。",
  "project": "symb_invariant_consensus"
}
```

\\\"\\\"\\\"Translate these premises into grounded Z3 boolean formulas. Each predicate-entity pair becomes a Bool variable (lowercase, underscores). Universal rules get grounded for every named entity.\\n\\nExample:\\nPremises: \\\"All managers work hard. If someone works hard, they are successful. Bob is a manager. Alice is not a manager.\\\"\\nEntities: bob, alice\\nVariables: manager_bob, manager_alice, works_hard_bob, works_hard_alice, successful_bob, successful_alice\\n\\nTranslation:\\n{\\\"variables\\\": [\\\"manager_bob\\\", \\\"manager_alice\\\", \\\"works_hard_bob\\\", \\\"works_hard_alice\\\", \\\"successful_bob\\\", \\\"successful_alice\\\"], \\\"premises\\\": [{\\\"nl\\\": \\\"All managers work hard [bob]\\\", \\\"z3\\\": \\\"Implies(manager_bob, works_hard_bob)\\\"}, {\\\"nl\\\": \\\"All managers work hard [alice]\\\", \\\"z3\\\": \\\"Implies(manager_alice, works_hard_alice)\\\"}, {\\\"nl\\\": \\\"If someone works hard, they are successful [bob]\\\", \\\"z3\\\": \\\"Implies(works_hard_bob, successful_bob)\\\"}, {\\\"nl\\\": \\\"If someone works hard, they are successful [alice]\\\", \\\"z3\\\": \\\"Implies(works_hard_alice, successful_alice)\\\"}, {\\\"nl\\\": \\\"Bob is a manager\\\", \\\"z3\\\": \\\"manager_bob == True\\\"}, {\\\"nl\\\": \\\"Alice is not a manager\\\", \\\"z3\\\": \\\"manager_alice == False\\\"}], \\\"entities\\\": [\\\"bob\\\", \\\"alice\\\"]}\\n\\nRules:\\n- \\\"All/If X then Y\\\" -> Implies(x_entity, y_entity) for EACH entity\\n- \\\"X or Y\\\" -> Or(x_entity, y_entity)\\n- \\\"X and Y\\\" -> And(x_entity, y_entity)\\n- \\\"not X\\\" -> Not(x_entity) or x_entity == False\\n- \\\"Either both A and B, or neither\\\" -> Or(And(A, B), And(Not(A), Not(B)))\\n- \\\"Either A or B (exclusive)\\\" -> Or(And(A, Not(B)), And(Not(A), B))\\n- Variable names: predicate_entity (e.g., tall_bob, likes_bob_alice)\\n- Z3 ops: Implies, And, Or, Not, == True, == False\\n- Do NOT use function calls like pred(x). \\\"temperature\\\": temperature,\\n    }\\n    try:\\n        resp = httpx.post(f\\\"{API_BASE}/chat/completions\\\", json=payload, timeout=120)\\n        resp.raise_for_status()\\n        return 'False', 'And', 'Or', 'Not', 'Implies', 'If', 'Xor', 'Bool'}\\n    var_names = tokens - reserved\\n\\n    ns = {}\\n    for name in var_names:\\n        ns[name] = z3.Bool(name)\\n    ns.update({\\n        'And': z3.And, 'Or': z3.Or, 'Not': z3.Not,\\n        'Implies': z3.Implies, 'If': z3.If, 'Xor': z3.Xor,\\n        'True': True, 'False': False,\\n    })\\n\\n    try:\\n        result = eval(s, return \\\"Error\\\"\\n\\n    # Check premises consistency\\n    s0 = z3.Solver()\\n    s0.set(\\\"timeout\\\", timeout_ms)\\n    for p in premise_z3_list:\\n        s0.add(p)\\n    if s0.check() == s2.set(\\\"timeout\\\", timeout_ms)\\n    for p in premise_z3_list:\\n        s2.add(p)\\n    s2.add(conclusion_z3)\\n    r2 = s2.check()\\n\\n    if r1 == z3.unsat:\\n        return \\\"True\\\"\\n raw_premise, parsed = blind_extract_premises(premises_text, temperature=TEMP_PREMISE)\\n    result[\\\"raw_premise_response\\\"] = raw_premise[:1500]\\n\\n    if parsed is None:\\n        return        return result\\n\\n    result[\\\"premise_extraction_success\\\"] = True\\n    result[\\\"num_premises_total\\\"] = len(premises_data)\\n\\n    premise_z3 = []\\n    premise_strs = []\\n    for <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The dry-run only completed 1/5 problems (folio_0) before apparently hanging or being killed. The first problem took 87.9 seconds. At this rate, 204 problems × ~88s = ~18000s ≈ 5 hours. The worker timed out well before that.

The results directory is empty, so no usable data was produced. The experiment is incomplete and failed.

Let me report this to the Director. The key information:
1. Worker failed due to extensive debugging (prompt iteration needed) + slow per-problem processing (~88s/problem at K=12)
2. Pipeline eventually worked (1/5 dry-run completed successfully: folio_0 correct, 12/12 valid traces)


3. A full run would take around 5 hours if I relaunched it as a background process, which is feasible. The approach itself is worth exploring though — translating premises to Z3 constraints without seeing the question, then evaluating the conclusion, tests whether answer-agnostic constraint extraction can reduce confirmation bias.
```

</details>

D085 实验锁定，论文写作冲刺\\n- **DDL**: EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25（5天）\\n- **实验**: 139 个（success 32 / negative 74 / failed 18），25+ 干预方法全部 negative\\n- **覆盖**: 3 模型家族 × 4 domains × 25+ 4.97x bias ratio, 83.3% 错误约束指向错误答案, 97.6% error overlap with SC\\n4. **Process-κ**: cosine similarity 0.69 >> answer-κ 0.41, r=-0.06, 仅 9.3% convergent trace pairs\\n5. **Independence-Accuracy Trade-off**: Debate κ 0.44→0.15 but accuracy -18.6pp（capstone finding）\\n6. **25+ Remediation ALL Failed**: prompt/architecture/capability/verification/diversity/filtering/method 全维度 → structural\\n7. _source line 2787 · 2026-05-20T09:33:55.930Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Direction F (Blind FOL) is running in the background. The worker reported:
- PID 378776, ~90-100s per problem
- Early trend: ALL predictions are "Unknown" — blind FOL constraints are insufficient for Z3 to determine entailment
- Auto-checkpoints every 10 problems

This "all Unknown" trend is concerning — it means the blind FOL approach systematically predicts Unknown because:
1. Without seeing the conclusion, the extracted premises-only FOL constraints don't contain enough information to determine True/False


2. Z3 can only determine satisfiability — without a conclusion to test against, it can't establish entailment and defaults to Unknown

If this pattern persists across all 204 problems, accuracy would plateau at whatever percentage of the ground truth is actually Unknown, around 20% for FOLIO. That would be a decisive failure. But with only 2 problems done so far, I should wait for checkpoint data before drawing conclusions.
```

</details>

### Assistant

_source line 2853 · 2026-05-20T10:17:52.055Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Direction K: Minority Report. The idea: when the minority of traces disagrees with the majority, check if the minority's FOL constraints are more internally consistent (Z3-satisfiable). If so, trust the minority.

Results:
- strict: Δ=0 (no flips at all — too conservative)
- moderate: Δ=-4.90pp (76 flips, only 27 correct = 35.5% precision — worse than random)
- combined: Δ=0 (12 flips, 6 correct / 6 wrong — net zero)


The minority traces are fundamentally less accurate than the majority at 33.73% versus 48.80% ground truth correctness, so relying on them backfires. Even when I filter by Z3 satisfiability, the 35.5% precision on flips stays well below random chance, confirming that constraint consistency doesn't actually correlate with correctness. This is another clear negative result showing that formal consistency metrics don't help distinguish right from wrong — the majority vote remains more reliable, which aligns with what the Condorcet Jury Theorem predicts when individual accuracy exceeds 50%.
```

</details>

{
  "text": "[severity: info] Direction K: Minority Report 审查\n\n**结论: PASS** — 干净的 negative result。\n\n### 核心验证\n\n- SC=54.41%, strict Δ=0, moderate Δ=-4.90pp, combined Δ=0 ✓\n- Moderate: 76 flips, 27 correct / 49 wrong, precision=35.5% (< 50%) ✓\n- Minority ground truth rate=33.73% vs majority=48.80% ✓\n- 运行 2.1s（纯分析，无 LLM 调用）✓\n\n### 关键发现\n\n**Minority traces 系统性地更不正确**（33.7% vs 48.8%）。这直接验证了 CJT 的预测：当单 voter accuracy > 50% 时，majority voting 是最优策略，minority 翻转只会损害 accuracy。\n\nConstraint consistency（Z3 satisfiability）作为\"哪方更可信\"的信号完全无效（precision 35.5%），与 Direction I (Z3 Filter, precision 49%) 和 Direction B (DeepConf, logprob 无区分力) 一致。\n\n### 对论文的价值\n\n一句话提及即可：\"Minority Report amplification — trusting the minority when its constraints are more Z3-consistent — degrades accuracy (−4.9pp), as minority traces are systematically less correct (33.7% vs 48.8%).\"\n\n进一步封堵 \"maybe we should trust the dissenting voice\" 这条 reviewer 可能提出的反驳。",

```text
This is a comprehensive status check. Let me review the key findings:

1. **Direction F (Blind FOL)**: Running, 53/204. Accuracy dropping (42% at 50, 39.6% at 53). Heavily biased toward Unknown predictions (75% Unknown correct but only 16.7% True and 25% False). This confirms my earlier prediction — blind FOL systematically predicts Unknown.

2. **Direction H (Adversarial)**: MISSING on westd-25068. But earlier a worker reported it running on westd-16639 at 57/204. 

I see the issue — the status check only looked at westd-25068 for Direction H, but the actual experiment is running on westd-16639, so there's a mismatch in which machine was checked.

3. **exp-075b (Contrastive Mistral FOLIO)**: Completed. Contrastive 47.55% vs SC 56.86%, Δ=-9.31pp. This is a previously known result — this experiment was already in the registry.

4. **exp-066 (Qwen-14B FOLIO seed123)**: Completed. SICA 75.0% vs SC 74.51%, Δ=+0.49pp. Also previously known.

5. **Direction G (Decoupling)**: Running on westd-16639 with only 10/204 problems completed for the conservative strategy, showing 70% accuracy so far. But running all 3 strategies across 204 problems will take over 10 hours total, which won't finish in time for the paper deadline.

The main blockers are the missed status check on the right machine for Direction H, Direction F showing a strong negative result, and Direction G taking too long to complete.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Pd4omnZ1CTGQ3N6dx1os1r`</summary>

```text
1	---
2	name: distilled-research
3	description: LLM+Symbolic Reasoning 领域研究经验——基线/数据集共识、方法趋势、实验设计、研究空白。25篇蒸馏。
4	type: skill
5	---
6	
7	## 基线与数据集共识
8	
9	**基线层级**（6/8，高）：CoT → SC-CoT → neurosymbolic (LINC/Logic-LM)。来源：Aristotle, DyLoS, Logic-LM。纯 prompting 不够作唯一 baseline。
10	
11	**数据集**（5/8，高）：FOLIO（5/8）是 naturalistic FOL 标准；ProofWriter（4/8）是 synthetic 标准；PrOntoQA（3/8）做 sanity check。AR-LSAT、LogicalDeduction 加多样性但非必需。
12	
13	**指标**：准确率是唯一共识，但领先论文加 Exe_Rate（Logic-LM）、soundness（Dissecting）、depth-stratified accuracy（4/8）。
14	
15	## 方法趋势
16	
17	**上升**（高）：单→多 solver 动态路由（DyLoS 4种solver）；process reward model（Math-Shepherd, V-STaR）；consistency-based 训练信号（CREST, CISC, DSC）。
18	
19	**消退**（高）：单 solver neurosymbolic 被多 solver 替代（DyLoS 直接超越 LINC/Logic-LM）；纯 CoT 作为方法。
20	
21	**新兴**（中）：约束编程集成（ConstraintLLM, LLM-meets-CP）；SAT-based 评测（SATBench）。
22	
23	## 实验设计共识
24	
25	**Depth analysis 必做**（5/8，高）：accuracy-vs-depth 曲线证明深层不退化。来源：LINC Fig4, Logic-LM Fig3, Aristotle Fig5。
26	
27	**Error taxonomy 加分**（4/8，高）：分 formalization / reasoning / interpretation error。LINC L1-L3 vs C1-C3（仅21/74错误重叠）是典型。
28	
29	**Cross-model 验证**（6/8，高）：至少 GPT-4 + 一个开源模型。ProverGen 测10+模型。
30	
31	## 竞品与局限
32	
33	**竞品 claim**：DyLoS—动态路由（+27%）；Aristotle—逻辑完备（99.7% one-step）；CREST—consistency > binary correctness。
34	
35	**高频局限**（高）：FOL 表达力不足（4/8）；形式化瓶颈占60-80%失败（3/8）；隐含知识盲区——Aristotle LogiQA 31.2% vs CoT 65.6%（2/8）。
36	
37	## 研究空白
38	
39	1. **Symbolic × debate = 零交集**（高）：debate 论文不用 symbolic，反之亦然
40	2. **跨表示一致性检验缺失**：无论文检查 FOL/SAT/CSP 对同一问题是否一致
41	3. **语义级 self-refinement 缺失**：Logic-LM refinement 只修语法不修语义（2/8）
42	4. **Symbolic 在 implicit reasoning 的差距**未被系统解决
43	
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}
5	\label{sec:condorcet}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
11	\end{equation}
12	In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. This \emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.
13	
14	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
15	\label{sec:pipeline}
16	
17	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
18	
19	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
20	
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}). The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:maxsat}
26	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
27	\end{equation}
28	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\texttt{answer\_a} == \texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.
29	
30	\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} = \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:
31	\begin{equation}
32	\label{eq:score}
33	\text{score}(a) = \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^*} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a) - \alpha \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a),
34	\end{equation}
35	where $\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \arg\max_{a} \text{score}(a)$.
36	
37	\paragraph{Degeneration under confirmation bias.} Self-extracted constraints are tightly coupled to their source answer. While ${\sim}$92\% are technically discriminative (Appendix~\ref{app:extraction}), their direction mirrors the model's answer distribution. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
38	\begin{equation}
39	\label{eq:degenerate}
40	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
41	\end{equation}
42	equivalent to majority voting. The ablation in Appendix~\ref{app:ablation} confirms this empirically.
43	
44	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0$ (fraction of questions with $>1$ distinct answer) is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC.
45	\begin{equation}
46	\label{eq:c0-def}
47	C_0 = \frac{|\{q \in \mathcal{Q} : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}|}{|\mathcal{Q}|}.
48	\end{equation}
49	
50	\begin{figure}[t]
51	\centering
52	\small
53	\begin{tikzpicture}[
54	    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,
55	    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,
56	                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\scriptsize},
57	    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},
58	    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},
59	    lbl/.style={font=\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},
60	    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,
61	                    inner sep=3pt},
62	]
63	\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};
64	\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\traces};
65	\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\constraints};
66	\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\ +\\weight};
67	\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\(Z3)};
68	
69	\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};
70	\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\vote};
71	
72	\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);
73	\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);
74	\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);
75	\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);
76	\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);
77	\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);
78	
79	\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};
80	
81	\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\scriptsize\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};
82	
83	\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};
84	\end{tikzpicture}
85	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
86	\label{fig:pipeline}
87	\end{figure}
88	
89	\subsection{Oracle Control Design}
90	\label{sec:oracle-design}
91	
92	To isolate whether the bottleneck lies in extraction or aggregation, we introduce an oracle control, following the compositionality-gap methodology of \citet{press2023compositionality}. Gold FOL annotations (FOLIO) or DSL rules (ProofWriter) replace self-extracted constraints; Z3 evaluates them against each candidate answer, with scoring unchanged (Eq.~\ref{eq:score}). If the oracle produces large gains while self-extraction does not, the failure localizes to extraction quality. Results appear in \S\ref{sec:oracle}.
93	
94	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
95	\label{sec:process-kappa-method}
96	
97	Answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ may underestimate the true independence bottleneck. Two traces that follow nearly identical reasoning can reach different final answers; $\kappa$ treats such pairs as disagreeing, overestimating independence. We quantify process-level similarity via pairwise cosine similarity over TF-IDF representations of chain-of-thought traces. For problem $q$ with $K$ traces:
98	\begin{equation}
99	\label{eq:process-sim}
100	\bar{s}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i, \mathbf{v}_j),
101	\end{equation}
102	where $\mathbf{v}_i$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$. When $\bar{s} \gg \kappa$, reasoning processes are more homogeneous than answers suggest. This metric applies to any trace aggregation method, not only SICA.
103	
104	\subsection{Scope}
105	\label{sec:scope}
106	
107	SICA uses \emph{same-model self-extraction}: the same LLM generates traces and extracts constraints. We test more than twenty remediation strategies: nine prompt-level spanning six dimensions, three method-level (ICG, Contrastive, Debate), and ten additional directions targeting the independence bottleneck from different angles (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). Methods using trained extractors, external provers, or multi-model ensembles fall outside scope.
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into FOL for external provers; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors. More recent systems extend this: DyLoS \citep{xu2026dylos} selects solver types, Aristotle \citep{li2025aristotle} applies logic-complete decomposition, FoVer \citep{wang2025fover} trains FOL verifiers, and LogicAgent \citep{luo2025logicagent} employs multi-perspective grounding. Each introduces capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity, consistent with our findings. Our oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models intensify this (QwQ-32B: $C_0 = 2.6\%$). Trained verifiers offer an alternative: outcome reward models \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}, process reward models \citep{lightman2024prm}, and Math-Shepherd \citep{wang2024mathshepherd}. Multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our symbolic confirmation bias finding (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}): the same model that produces an error cannot reliably generate constraints that correct it.
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} establishes that independent majority voting converges to truth. \citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM ensemble classification. We extend this to reasoning-trace aggregation, measuring $\kappa$ and $K_{\text{eff}}$ across 15 conditions (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
```

1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	The regime-dependent pattern in \S\ref{sec:main-results} establishes \emph{where} SICA succeeds and fails. This section investigates \emph{why}: trace correlation quantifies the SC plateau (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), symbolic confirmation bias identifies the root cause (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence reveals a deeper bottleneck (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics confirm the mechanism (\S\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and more than twenty remediation strategies demonstrate a fundamental independence-accuracy trade-off (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. Qwen3-14B in thinking mode reaches $\kappa = 0.91$ ($K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$). The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ ($K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.8\text{--}2.2$), yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence, drives the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ (Qwen conditions with $K_{\text{eff}} \leq 1.30$ never improve) and sufficient SC accuracy (Mistral on ProofWriter with the highest $K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ but SC $= 39\%$ produces significant degradation). Even when both hold, sampling variance determines whether the constraint pool is discriminative. Cross-model validation with three families confirms universality: Mistral-7B ($\kappa = 0.44$), LLaMA-3.1-8B ($\kappa = 0.41$), Qwen2.5-7B ($\kappa = 0.66$), and Qwen2.5-14B ($\kappa = 0.74$) all produce $\kappa$ well above zero (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).

40	
41	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
42	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
43	
44	The correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.
45	
46	The 12 instances where SICA corrects SC errors reveal the mechanism's boundary: 43\% of these flips occur when a minority trace provides gold-aligned constraints, suggesting constraint diversity---not MAX-SAT---drives the rare successes. The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with that answer; aggregation over such constraints reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}).
47	
48	Iterative refinement does not break this cycle. CEGIS achieves $\Delta = +3.43$\,pp in round~1 but oscillates in rounds~2--5 without convergence, as each step reintroduces model bias. Cross-model extraction (Qwen3-14B or Qwen2.5-14B extracting from Mistral-7B traces) produces accuracy within $\pm 1$\,pp of self-extraction---the bias is a property of LLM formalization itself. The oracle ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that 75.9\% of the potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality, localizing confirmation bias as the binding bottleneck.
49	
50	
51	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
52	\label{sec:process-kappa}
53	
54	Answer-level $\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), the mean pairwise cosine similarity of chain-of-thought traces (TF-IDF, Eq.~\ref{eq:process-sim}) is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.41$. These measures are uncorrelated ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$): problems with diverse answers do not exhibit diverse reasoning. We find 9.3\% of trace pairs converge on reasoning ($\cos > 0.8$) while disagreeing on the final answer---apparent answer diversity masking process-level homogeneity.
55	
56	This gap implies that interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) may spread answers without altering reasoning paths. Process-level correlation establishes a tighter bound on effective ensemble diversity than answer-level $\kappa$ alone.
57	
58	
59	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
60	\label{sec:diagnostics}
61	
62	Four analyses confirm the mechanism (details in Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). Extraction taxonomy: 91.8\% of constraints are discriminative, but their direction mirrors the majority; 42.9\% are redundant across traces. Constraint ablation ($n = 804$): MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4\% of predictions---the solver adds no value over counting because constraint weights carry information redundant with vote counts. Hyperparameter sensitivity: $K = 12$ is the only setting with $\Delta > 2$\,pp among five values; $T = 0.7$ the only temperature with a positive effect---peaks coincide with low SC baselines. Entropy decomposition: all effects concentrate in high-entropy problems (up to $+12.8$\,pp), but direction depends on accuracy regime.
63	
64	
65	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
66	\label{sec:remediation}
67	\label{sec:interventions}
68	
69	We test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.
70	
71	\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\% to 6.0\%, but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\Delta = -1.1$\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models yield 0\% discriminative constraints; stronger models produce accuracy within $\pm 1$\,pp of self-extraction. Multi-temperature sampling ($T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$) yields $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
72	
73	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from premises. Despite producing superior constraints---higher Z3 compilation, lower redundancy---ICG yields $\Delta \approx 0$ across three seeds ($p = .206$, n.s.). Per-constraint-set reranking produces $\kappa = 0.47$, nearly identical to SC's $\kappa = 0.44$: the bottleneck is a property of the model itself. Contrastive scoring yields $\Delta = -9.31$\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$); 62.7\% of constraints are trivially satisfied. Debate produces $\Delta = -18.63$ and $-23.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets), with discordant pairs heavily skewed toward correct$\to$wrong flips. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, all negative.
74

79	\label{tab:method_interventions}

93	
94	\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments confirm the pattern (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Formal verification via code generation (PROVE) achieves only 15.2\% compilable programs with $\Delta = -1.9$\,pp. Iterative CEGIS refinement oscillates after round~1 without convergence. Logprob-based filtering (DeepConf) finds near-zero separation between correct and incorrect traces ($\Delta_{\text{logprob}} = 0.006$). Cross-model SICA with three families reduces $\kappa$ from 0.53 to 0.42 but yields $\Delta = -0.49$\,pp ($p = 1.0$). Problem perturbation \emph{increases} $\kappa$ to 0.70 (anti-effect). MIN-UNSAT objective produces $\Delta = -6.5$\,pp. $\kappa$-aware weighted voting yields $\Delta = 0$ across 11 conditions. Z3-based trace filtering achieves 49.1\% precision with $\Delta = -0.49$\,pp. Bootstrapped gold constraints oscillate like CEGIS. ASCII format perturbation yields negligible $\kappa$ reduction (0.39 $\to$ 0.36).
95	
96	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO and adds SC from three model families. SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling. Debate breaks through to $\kappa = 0.15$ ($K_{\text{eff}} = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. All three model families cluster above $\kappa = 0.40$, confirming the ceiling is architecture-independent (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). This constitutes a Pareto frontier (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}): methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Cross-model SICA (Mistral+LLaMA+Qwen, $K{=}12$) provides direct evidence: mixing three families reduces $\kappa$ from 0.53 to 0.42 yet yields $\Delta = -0.49$\,pp ($p = 1.0$)---even cross-architecture diversity is insufficient within the self-extraction paradigm. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.
97

SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s42) & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nThe correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.\n\nThe 12 instances where SICA corrects SC errors reveal the mechanism's boundary: 43\\% of these flips occur when a minority trace provides gold-aligned constraints, suggesting constraint diversity---not MAX-SAT---drives the rare successes. The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with that answer; aggregation over such constraints reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nIterative refinement does not break this cycle. CEGIS achieves $\\Delta = +3.43$\\,pp in round~1 but oscillates in rounds~2--5 without convergence, as each step reintroduces model bias. Cross-model extraction (Qwen3-14B or Qwen2.5-14B extracting from Mistral-7B traces) produces accuracy within $\\pm 1$\\,pp of self-extraction---the bias is a property of LLM formalization itself. The oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that 75.9\\% of the potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality, localizing confirmation bias as the binding bottleneck.\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), the mean pairwise cosine similarity of chain-of-thought traces (TF-IDF, Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}) is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. These measures are uncorrelated ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$): problems with diverse answers do not exhibit diverse reasoning. We find 9.3\\% of trace pairs converge on reasoning ($\\cos > 0.8$) while disagreeing on the final answer---apparent answer diversity masking process-level homogeneity.\n\nThis gap implies that interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) may spread answers without altering reasoning paths. Process-level correlation establishes a tighter bound on effective ensemble diversity than answer-level $\\kappa$ alone.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFour analyses confirm the mechanism (details in Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). Extraction taxonomy: 91.8\\% of constraints are discriminative, but their direction mirrors the majority; 42.9\\% are redundant across traces. Constraint ablation ($n = 804$): MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4\\% of predictions---the solver adds no value over counting because constraint weights carry information redundant with vote counts. Hyperparameter sensitivity: $K = 12$ is the only setting with $\\Delta > 2$\\,pp among five values; $T = 0.7$ the only temperature with a positive effect---peaks coincide with low SC baselines. Entropy decomposition: all effects concentrate in high-entropy problems (up to $+12.8$\\,pp), but direction depends on accuracy regime.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\\% to 6.0\\%, but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models yield 0\\% discriminative constraints; stronger models produce accuracy within $\\pm 1$\\,pp of self-extraction. Multi-temperature sampling ($T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$) yields $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from premises. Despite producing superior constraints---higher Z3 compilation, lower redundancy---ICG yields $\\Delta \\approx 0$ across three seeds ($p = .206$, n.s.). Per-constraint-set reranking produces $\\kappa = 0.47$, nearly identical to SC's $\\kappa = 0.44$: the bottleneck is a property of the model itself. Contrastive scoring yields $\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$); 62.7\\% of constraints are trivially satisfied. Debate produces $\\Delta = -18.63$ and $-23.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets), with discordant pairs heavily skewed toward correct$\\to$wrong flips. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, all negative.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method-level interventions on Mistral-7B with McNemar's paired test. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds; $p$ for the first. {*}{*}{*}\\,$p < 0.001$; {*}{*}\\,$p < 0.01$.}\n\\label{tab:method_interventions}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll rc rc@{}}\n\\toprule\n& & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{FOLIO ($n = 204$)} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{LogiQA ($n = 200$)} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){3-4} \\cmidrule(lr){5-6}\nMethod & Target & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC baseline & --- & 0.00 & --- & 0.00 & --- \\\\\nICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96\\,/\\,$+$1.47 & .206 & $-$0.50 & .706 \\\\\nContrastive & Scoring bias & $\\mathbf{-9.31}$ & .007 & $-$4.50 & .160 \\\\\nDebate & Role diversity & $\\mathbf{-18.63}$ & ${<}.001$ & $\\mathbf{-23.00}$ & ${<}.001$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments confirm the pattern (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Formal verification via code generation (PROVE) achieves only 15.2\\% compilable programs with $\\Delta = -1.9$\\,pp. Iterative CEGIS refinement oscillates after round~1 without convergence. Logprob-based filtering (DeepConf) finds near-zero separation between correct and incorrect traces ($\\Delta_{\\text{logprob}} = 0.006$). Cross-model SICA with three families reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.53 to 0.42 but yields $\\Delta = -0.49$\\,pp ($p = 1.0$). Problem perturbation \\emph{increases} $\\kappa$ to 0.70 (anti-effect). MIN-UNSAT objective produces $\\Delta = -6.5$\\,pp. $\\kappa$-aware weighted voting yields $\\Delta = 0$ across 11 conditions. Z3-based trace filtering achieves 49.1\\% precision with $\\Delta = -0.49$\\,pp. Bootstrapped gold constraints oscillate like CEGIS. ASCII format perturbation yields negligible $\\kappa$ reduction (0.39 $\\to$ 0.36).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO and adds SC from three model families. SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling. Debate breaks through to $\\kappa = 0.15$ ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All three model families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$, confirming the ceiling is architecture-independent (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). This constitutes a Pareto frontier (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}): methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Cross-model SICA (Mistral+LLaMA+Qwen, $K{=}12$) provides direct evidence: mixing three families reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.53 to 0.42 yet yields $\\Delta = -0.49$\\,pp ($p = 1.0$)---even cross-architecture diversity is insufficient within the self-extraction paradigm. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \\citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off (FOLIO, $n = 204$). Top: aggregation methods on Mistral-7B; bottom: SC across three model families. All families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$, confirming an architecture-independent ceiling. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.5pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Aggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\\ (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\\\\n& Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & 54.41 \\\\\n& Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\\\\n& ICG & 0.47 & 1.93 & 10 & 51.96 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & Self-Consistency & 0.41 & 2.17 & 12 & 66.18 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & Self-Consistency & 0.66 & 1.46 & 12 & 75.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Self-Consistency & 0.74 & 1.32 & 12 & 75.49 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",
